## Links, by PDF and source group

Logical marks count each source macro once, including nested doubts which share their enclosing hyperlink. PDF annotations can be more numerous because one marked passage can wrap across lines. The audit compares every shipped logical mark with the source expectation and independently checks each source key/URL pair against the archive PDF-page metadata.

## Ink boundaries

Every final PDF page was raster-screened at 144 dpi and its drawing/text bounding boxes checked for objects outside the sheet. Candidate components were then inspected as enlarged crops. The body is 18 mm from the left, 45 mm from the right, 18 mm from the top and 22 mm from the bottom; the 28 mm marginal-note column begins 7 mm beyond the body, and page numbers/headers occupy their declared regions. A 0.5 pt raster quantization tolerance is recorded. Only the permitted overhang of one letter remains; punctuation overhangs and layout collisions were repaired locally.

## Visual-review coverage and provenance

The three readers each include a forty-page sample evenly spread over the complete PDF. Every source page identified by the retained TeX overflow history was additionally selected in each reader, including all its continuation pages; every landscape page was also selected. The full guide is covered, including every previously overflowing heading and bottom-ink location. Selection and rendering alone are never counted as visual inspection.

The separate historical raster audit identified **382 bottom-overflow PDF sheets**. Each was mapped by source key and continuation offset to the final corrected-apparatus PDF, producing 382 distinct final target pages. Every target has validated visual-review coverage. These historical sheets are additional to the source-page overflow records and are not described as current defects. The complete mapping is `records/QA_SELECTION_PS_EN_corrected_apparatus.json`.

A direct review means an image rendered from the exact current PDF was opened and observed. An inherited review uses the method “raster identity with an actually opened image”: an original observation remains attached to its old PDF and retained PNG, and an audit proves equality with the current page at 144 dpi and at the original 1.4 rendering scale. The gate validates the current and old PDF/image hashes, the immutable original evidence snapshot and entry, the equivalence-audit hash, geometry, pixel signatures, and normalized historical provenance. When an original record did not store a PNG byte hash, that absence remains explicit; its retained PNG must instead be bound to the exact old PDF raster. Any changed raster page requires a fresh direct current-image opening.

The full pre-packaging gate runs with every old PDF present and verifies its bytes. Final delivery explicitly supersedes those intermediate PDFs, so extracted verification uses `--allow-omitted-historical-pdfs --check-only` after image-alias restoration. That mode accepts an absent predecessor only through its exact `FINAL_OMISSIONS.tsv` row, validates all preserved PNG/original-record/audit hashes, and freshly renders the current page at both required scales. It does not claim to reread an absent old PDF or to reopen the inherited image visually. Any old PDF actually present still receives normal hash and geometry checks.

### Annotated-master body reuse

Every master body page, physical pages 2–1195, was compared with the corresponding corrected-apparatus page at 144 dpi. All 1,194 pairs had identical geometry, decoded RGB bytes and lossless PNG bytes. This supports reuse of the 477 corresponding apparatus pages with validated visual review; it does not claim that every master body page was opened. The distinct master title/front page was opened directly from the final PDF. The complete proof is `records/MASTER_APPARATUS_RASTER_EQUIVALENCE.json`. Audit SHA-256: `c457eafc325af99e3114607167497f1a4eaac955d900595426e4495ee21f941b`. The comparison itself claims zero image openings.

Original observations, including any defects described on superseded PDFs, remain in their source records and immutable historical snapshots. They are not relabeled as current-PDF openings or counted as unresolved current defects. `FINAL_QA_GATE.json` lists the superseded evidence separately; current coverage is accepted only through a validated direct opening or the explicit raster-equality chain above.

## Exact change replay

All 706 of 706 source pages reconstruct identically, with complete source coverage. The audit exposes 1 historical explanatory record already incorporated by exact edit rows; its full row objects and canonical row hashes are bound in `records/REPLAY_RECORD_ADJUDICATIONS.json` (SHA-256 `adbec23fa1459fc26b735f9fd43f55ca35e3f0490f503b9362a91d49dfcf1c8d`). There are 0 unexplained unmatched records, 0 adjudication issues and 0 replay exceptions. Any such unresolved condition fails the final gate.

## Historical repairs and supersession

The retained overflow history contains 268 measured events, with a largest recorded excess of 178.40779pt. These include failed and superseded builds; they are not final defects. Every event remains in `records/OVERFLOW_HISTORY.tsv`; local repairs, source lines and their reasons are in `records/TYPESETTING_FIXES.tsv`. The final boxes are all at most 2 pt.

There is no blanket rescaling. The specifically recorded whole-diagram reduction is 82% on 134-6/106; smaller scale values attached to decorative marks are not reductions of text or mathematical diagrams. Long displays are locally broken, and diagram gaps are adjusted at their source. Long marginal notes are ruled, labeled blocks at their call; short notes use the margin queue.

The checkpoint inventory covers 71 verified archives, 5972 paths and 8231 distinct member versions. Every inventoried logical path has a retained counterpart or an explicit replacement; duplicate-image paths are recoverable through the alias manifest. The complete file-by-file disposition, including every superseded passage-bearing version, is `records/CHECKPOINT_SUPERSESSION.tsv`. Earlier partial reader, guide, record and report versions are superseded by the named final counterparts; historical diagnostics are retained with runtime-location redactions documented separately. No earlier checkpoint was overwritten or deleted.

## Extraction, image aliases and checkpoint save status

Extract the English and records packages into the same working directory, preserving their folders. The packages store each distinct image byte sequence once. `records/FILE_ALIASES.tsv` maps every omitted duplicate-image path to a retained image with identical bytes and its SHA-256. Before running record or proof verification, run `python3 scripts/restore_file_aliases.py` from the extracted project directory. The script checks the retained bytes against the manifest before recreating the duplicate paths. This restores every logical proof path at its original content and resolution. Core sources, PDFs and records remain physical archive members.

Checkpoint 67 was validated locally (591,029,848 bytes), but its save preparation was rejected before any upload because the file exceeded 512 MiB. The local ZIP is preserved and is not represented as durably saved. Checkpoint 68 was durably uploaded; its ZIP CRC passed and all 817 aliases were restored exactly. Two physical members nevertheless disagreed with the manifest because they changed between hashing and ZIP writing: `scripts/write_final_reports.py` and `records/MATH_PROSE_PROOF_RENDER_MANIFEST.json`. The immutable archive and its actual member versions remain inventoried, and subsequent full checkpoints capture stable bytes before hashing and packaging and explicitly supersede those race-affected entries. The complete older-version and explicit-replacement retention manifests remain in force.

Checkpoint 68 has no detected content loss: all 5,851 logical files are accounted for through 5,034 physical members and 817 verified aliases. Its two stale checksum declarations and the actual archived byte hashes are distinguished in `records/CHECKPOINT_68_LOGICAL_MEMBER_AUDIT.json`; the restoration audit is `records/CHECKPOINT_68_RESTORATION_AUDIT.json`. `CHECKPOINT_ALIAS_MEMBERS.tsv` records each checkpoint alias separately, and `CHECKPOINT_MANIFEST_VERIFICATION.json` distinguishes documented historical metadata discrepancies from unexplained failures.

## Intermediate-PDF supersession

Only the five published PDFs are included in final delivery. `records/FINAL_OMISSIONS.tsv` maps 59 exact intermediate-PDF versions to their final-reader replacements, with both SHA-256 values and individual reasons. This includes clean viewed local proofs as well as obsolete cote/probe builds and predecessor PDFs used during raster verification. Their non-PDF sources, logs, PNG observations and provenance remain available, and all original workspace/checkpoint files remain unchanged. `CHECKPOINT_SUPERSESSION.tsv` classifies the omitted paths as explicitly superseded for final delivery, rather than retained PDFs. `records/FINAL_OMISSIONS_AUDIT.json` records source scope and associated evidence.

## Final archive verification

Verify the complete published pair with `python3 scripts/verify_final_archives.py PATH_EN.zip PATH_RECORDS.zip --restore --final-gate`. The verifier must restore the exact declared alias set and run the final publication gate. Within an already extracted project, use `python3 scripts/final_qa_gate.py --allow-omitted-historical-pdfs --check-only`; this checks the explicit intermediate-PDF supersessions without rewriting the archived gate record. A generic checkpoint manifest/restoration check without `--final-gate` certifies only the checkpoint manifest and restoration properties, not final publication.
